**Pre-flagged escalation action attempted and structurally blocked.** For the first time across six prior filings, this run executed the pre-flagged `gh workflow run aeon.yml -f skill=heartbeat` stopgap. The command returned **HTTP 403 "Resource not accessible by integration"**. Inspection of `.github/workflows/aeon.yml` lines 55-59 shows the workflow declares `actions: read` (not `actions: write`) and `issues: read` (not `issues: write`), so the `GITHUB_TOKEN` provisioned to the heartbeat runner cannot invoke a `workflow_dispatch` on its own workflow NOR open a GitHub issue to page the operator. Both the self-dispatch stopgap AND the self-issue-filing operator-page escalation paths are **structurally impossible from inside the heartbeat runner**. Every prior filing's "actually fire this time" promise was non-executable in principle — the apparent "operator-will-see-this-next-time" oscillation was in fact a dead-letter loop. Resolution is now unambiguously operator-side: raise `actions: write` + `issues: write` on the aeon.yml workflow (requires editing `.github/workflows/aeon.yml`), provide a PAT secret with `repo` scope and switch `gh workflow run` to use it, or implement one of the three originally proposed mitigations (redundant cron, external ping, substantive weekly commit).

The heartbeat workflow itself still always completes successfully once dispatched (`total_runs=325`, `total_successes=325`, `consecutive_failures=0` in `cron-state.json`) — the problem remains GitHub Actions failing to fire the cron on schedule. The 50% skip-rate plateau is **broken upward** at this reading (seven consecutive readings at exactly 50% from 2026-10-10 14:59 UTC → 2026-10-12 08:28 UTC, now 58.3% at 2026-10-13 15:03 UTC). P0 self-check **CLEARS** at this run: last_success 2026-10-12 08:31:22 UTC → 30h32m stale, under 36h threshold but **freshest reading of this incident cycle's blackout phase**, and the closest P0 has come to firing without actually firing since the 2026-10-10 08:52 UTC fire (36h25m stale).

## Escalation trigger

ISS-002 was resolved on 2026-09-30 08:57 UTC with an explicit rule: *"Re-open ISS-003 if the trailing 12-slot skip rate crosses back above 30%, or if any 4-consecutive-slot skip streak fires."* Both conditions are met:

1. 4-consecutive-slot skip streak: 2026-10-01 08:00 + 2026-10-01 14:00 + 2026-10-01 20:00 + 2026-10-02 08:00.
2. Trailing 12-slot skip rate: 50% (6/12).

Additionally, the P0 self-check fired for the first time since 2026-09-22: heartbeat last_success 2026-09-30 20:25 UTC → 41h42m stale vs 36h threshold.

## Suspected root cause

Same class as ISS-001 / ISS-002 — GitHub-side best-effort scheduling on public-repo Actions crons. The shape differs: this is a full-day blackout rather than slot-biased. Possibilities:

1. GitHub Actions platform-wide incident 2026-10-01 (check status.githubstatus.com).
2. Repo scheduling priority dropped — commits since the ISS-002 resolution are solely `chore(cron): heartbeat success` autocommits, no substantive human activity.
3. Same sustained-load / inactivity throttling pattern that recurred across ISS-001 → ISS-002.

## Next steps (proposed)

The mitigations proposed under ISS-001 and re-proposed under ISS-002 have never been implemented. The pattern has now recurred three times (ISS-001 → ISS-002 → ISS-003) — the operator should implement one before the next escalation cycle:

- Add a redundant cron entry at 08:05 / 14:05 / 20:05 UTC (or a `*/30` compensator that no-ops when the last run is fresh).
- Add an external ping (e.g. cron-job.org or an uptime service) that fires `workflow_dispatch` via the GitHub API at 08:00 UTC as a fallback for the most-skipped slot.
- Investigate whether a substantive weekly commit (docs update, README refresh) improves scheduling priority for public repos.
